NEWCHWANG. 3 X (Received 14th April 1902.) CUSTOM HOUSE, NEWCHWANG, 8th April 1902. SIR, I.&mdash;In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the first quarter of the year 1902. 1902.] NEWCHWANG. 7 * r . CUSTOM HOUSE, NEWCHWANG, 8th April 1902. SIR, I.&mdash;The Imperial Russian Provisional Civil Administration having opened, on the 12th August 1900, a Custom House to collect, under Foreign supervision, Dues and Likin on Junk Cargoes, I now transmit the statistics of that trade from 1st January to 31st March 1902. On all cargoes arriving or leaving by sea-going junks Dues and Duties were collected at Treaty Tariff rates, and Likin at the rate of ^ per cent, ad valorem on Imports and ^ per cent, ad valorem on Exports, with the exception of the Duties on certain specified staples, which were reduced on 1st May 1901 to the rate formerly assessed by the Native Customs. Boats trading up river paid Tonnage Dues, but no Duties were levied on their cargoes. II. TONNAGE.&mdash;The number and movements of sea-going junks, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Table:&mdash; January. February. March. Total. No. Piculs. No. Piculs. No. ! Piculs. No. Piculs. ___ _____ 1 I Inwards ... ... ... 81 43,035 81 43,035 Outwards ... ... ... 108 30,153 108 30,153 ____| 1 1 lr 1 Of Junks entered&mdash; Of Junks cleared&mdash; * # 24 were from Shanhaikwan. 7 were for Shanhaikwan. 30 &bdquo; &bdquo; Chinchow-fu. 3 &bdquo; &bdquo; Chinchow-fu. 21 &bdquo; &bdquo; Kaichow. &#39; 16 *&bdquo; &bdquo; Kaichow. 1 was &bdquo; Shantung Ports. 82 &bdquo; &bdquo; Shantung Ports. 5 were &bdquo; Shanghai. 81 Entered. 108 Cleared. 37 up-river boats paid Tonnage Dues during the quarter. 395 up-river boats and sea-going junks registered during the quarter. III. IMPORTS.&mdash;There were no Imports from Foreign Countries by sea-going junks during the quarter. IV. IMPORTS.&mdash;There were no Imports of Foreign origin from Chinese Ports by sea-going junks during the quarter. &bull;&#39;

210 CSO.G 11[JAN.-MAR., 1902.1 NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 371. YANGTZE RIVER-HANKOW DISTRICT. HAVOC ROCKS BEACON-LIGHT ESTABLISHED. NOTICE is hereby given that the Havoc Rocks 3eacon-light was exhibited for the first time on the I5th December 1901. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, of the sixth order, showing a fixed white light. The beacon is placed on the blunt point on the right bank of the river, immediately above the Havoc Rocks. SHANGI, I I th FebruaiT 1902.

226 CvaTOMS, GAZErrE. [JAN.-MAB.,, (ROCeivd 2st April 19o2.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 14Tn APRIL 1902. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the first quarter of the year 1902. b. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are Taishan ( 1), Lintin ( fT), and Shamchun( ), on the north and west; Samun (= fq), on the east; and Shaiichung ($ g ) and Shatowkok (on ),onthe Mirs Bay littoral. The Custom House is called ft c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwar&#39;ds,&quot; from China.- II. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos..21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and f 1875, 1 now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this por or the first quarter of the year 1902. b. The Custom House ( ) was opened on the ut July I899. The collection of Tariff Import an lport Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Th 30 + 8o per picul), and the collection of Native Duties an ikin on Junk Cargoes at half Tariff rates, commenced on that day,. The stations at present provisionally coitrolle4 y the Kiaocipw Customs are known as Taputon (u S $); Hungshilhyi (f ) Lingshanwe M [ii #;), including Tachiangkou (;ft ); Nuku ( ); Tsangkou ( E); and Shatezkou (9 f U iclding Tengyao ( ). c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China, th tatistics of goods landed and&#39;consumed in German territory not being included.
